TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
TROY BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
TO: Board of Education 
FROM:  Richard M. Machesky, Ed.D. 
RE: Agenda Memo for the Organizational Meeting, January 17, 2017 
1. ELECTION OF ACTING CHAIRPERSON 
The first order of business is to e lect an acting chairperson who will 
conduct the meeting until such time as a president has been elected.  
Past practice has been that the previous year’s president conducts this 
part of the meeting. 
2. APPOINTMENT OF TEMPORARY SECRETARY 
 The second order of business is for the acting chairperson  to 
 appoint Judy Holmes to act as temporary secretary until such time as the 
 secretary to the Board has been elected. 
3. ELECTION OF PRESIDENT 
The acting chairperson declares nominations in order for the office o f 
president of the board of education (please note, nominations need not 
be seconded; and a motion and support are made to close the 
nomination.)  Please understand that four votes are required for election. 
4. ELECTION OF VICE PRESIDENT 
The newly elected  president declares nominations in order for the 
office of vice president, following the same procedures as outlined for the 
election of president. 
5. ELECTION OF A SECRETARY 
The president declares nominations in order for the office of secretary, 
following the same procedures as outlined for the election of president. 
6. APPOINTMENT OF TREASURER 
According to Board Policy, the treasurer is appointed annually by the 
Board as a nonvoting officer.  The assistant superintendent of business 
services has served  as treasurer of the Board.  It is my recommendation 
that this procedure continue.

         4. C.        01.17.17 
WHEREAS, there may be in and may, hereafter from time to time, come into 
the hands of Rick West, Treasurer of Troy School District, certain public monies 
belonging to or held for the State, County, or other political units of the State or 
otherwise held according to law, and 
WHEREAS, under the laws of Michigan, this Board is required to provide by 
Resolution for the deposit of all public monies, including tax monies coming into 
the hands of said Treasurer, on one or more banks, hereafter called bank(s) to 
be designated in such Resolution. 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that said Treasurer, Rick West, is hereby 
directed to deposit and/or invest all public monies, including tax monies now in 
or coming into his hands as Treasurer in his name as Treasurer, into the 
following depository institutions: 
Bank of America 
Bank of New York 
Charter One Bank 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Comerica Bank 
First Merit Bank  
Fifth Third Bank 
Flagstar Bancorp 
MILAF (Michigan Liquid Asset Fund Plus, managed 
By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank) 
Morgan Stanley Smith Barney 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
PNC Bank 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District may be 
withdrawn by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, Rick 
West, and in emergencies the handwritten signature of the Treasurer, Rick West, 
or the Superintendent, Richard M. Machesky, who may sign on the General Fund 
account and the Payroll account at Bank of America; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging t o the District for the 
accounts established at PNC Bank  for purposes of paying Troy Career Center 
expenses may be withdrawn by checks with the handwritten signature of The 
Troy Career Center Manager, Assistant Manager, or Program Coordinators; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the 
account established at Comerica Bank for purposes of paying health care and 
dependent care flexible spending claims may be withdrawn by ACH debit to Next 
Generation Enrollment, Inc., or by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the 
Treasurer, Rick West, and 
11
         4. C.        01.17.17 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer, Rick West, may open or close 
accounts with the above -listed institutions, provided it is in the interest of the 
District; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that funds of the District may be invested 
according to Board Policy 5000.06; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer is authorized to combine 
money from more than one fund for the purpose of making an investment 
authorized by  subsection (1) (h) of Section 380.1223 of the Revised School 
Code; and 
THE MAXIMUM AMOUNT TO BE INVESTED with the above institutions is 
regulated by the school code. 
I, ______________________, Secretary of Troy School District, Oakland County, 
Michigan, certify that the Resolution was adopted by said Board at a meeting 
thereof held on the date specified above. 
Given under my hand and seal of the Troy School District in the County of 
Oakland in the State of Michigan this 17th day of January 2017.

      5. A.   01.17.17 
5.  RECOGNITION       
A.    Board of Education Recognition 
Each January, the Troy School District recognizes and celebrates the 
work of a very important group of individuals: The Board of 
Education.  
Troy is truly fortunate to be governed by Board members who  
dedicate an incredible amount of time and energy toward ensuring 
that we provide the best possible educational opportunities for all 
students. You are tireless, professional, and passionate  and this 
community owes you a debt of gratitude for all that you do and all 
that you give. T onight, we would like to recognize our Board of 
Education members with a gift that represents our students and staff 
- the very people you work so hard to serve. 
One of the first initiatives we developed when you selected me to 
lead this district was to develop the idea of ONE TROY —that we are 
not merely a district of schools, but ONE school district, with a single 
mission and a single focus on student achievement for ALL. 
This evening, we are happy to present you each with a ONE Troy 
ensemble, a basket filled with a token of appreciation and pride in 
each of our schools. You will find t -shirts and hats, water bottles and 
mugs, pens and pencils—there are even some baked goods in there, 
each serving as a symbol of one of our outstanding schools. We hope 
that this will serve as a reminder of the impact you have on the l ives 
of our students and staff. 
From our more senior members to our newest trustees, we are 
grateful to have each and every one of you. 
Thank you for all you do.   
Recommended Action:  Present gift to Board members.

BOARD MEETING   MINUTES DECEMBER 20, 2016 
    SERVICES BUILDING  
 President Philippart called the meeting to order at  7:0 0 
p.m. 
In addition to Dr. Philippart, present were Board 
members Edmunds, Fleming, Hauff, Schmidt, and 
Underwood. Mr. Miletti was absent.  Superintendent 
Machesky was also absent. All four high school stud ent 
representatives were also in attendance.  Also pres ent 
were Deputy Superintendent Mark Dziatczak; 
administrators Birmingham, Griffor, Harris, Carson,  
Gottlieb, and West; and approximately 50 additional  
members of the audience.   
RECOGNITION  
Years of Dedicated Service to 
the Troy Board of Education: 
Ida Edmunds and Wendy 
Board members, Wendy Underwood and Ida Edmunds, 
who are retiring from the Troy Board of Education.  Dr. 
Philippart announced that this evening was their fi nal 
meeting. It’s hard to describe the impact these two  
trustees have had on our community, but it has been  
tremendous. Wendy is leaving us after selflessly gi ving 
more than 11 years of service to our students, staff and 
families. Ida is retiring after dedicating more tha n 31 
years of her time, talents and passion into learnin g for 
all. Aside from being an elected position, most of you 
know that serving on the Board of Education is also  a 
volunteer position, and it requires a great deal of  time, 
effort and commitment. These two exemplary trustees  
have done a remarkable service to past generations 
and future generations of Troy students. We know th is 
is not goodbye, and they will no doubt be active in  the 
greater Troy community…. but on this Board, will mi ss 
them.  Tonight, we have several declarations to rea d in 
recognition of these two trustees.   
Dr. Philippart presented both Mrs. Edmunds and Ms. 
Underwood with a framed letter from Senator Gary 
Peters’ office and a gift from the Troy School Dist rict 
and School Board for their years of service.  
23
Underwood 
This evening, the Board recognized two of their own  
Oakland County Commissioner, Wade Fleming, was 
invited up to share some kind words about Ms. 
Underwood and Mrs. Edmunds and thank them for their 
years of service to the Troy community.  He present ed 
each with a proclamation signed by members of the 
Oakland County Board of Commissioners.

Student Representatives to 
International Academy-East, announced that students  
have been busy taking mid- term exams all week.

Personal Project Exhibition for the MYP Middle Year s 
Program on January 13 th  from 9 – 11:00 a.m. and 
invited everyone to attend.   
Parker Gilchrist, Troy College & Career High School , 
announced that Student Government sponsored a 
clothing drive to benefit Grace Centers for Hope in  
Pontiac.  They also celebrated Diversity and Inclus ion 
Day by wearing t-shirts and bracelets.  Leadership and 
Social Justice Project students will begin practicing their 
“Fingers Crossed” skits to present to middle schools this 
spring.  
Pranathi Locula, student representative from Troy H igh 
School, reported that Troy High was having a dodgeball 
tournament to support research for mast cell diseas e, 
which one of their teachers has. Pranathi also shar ed 
that they embraced diversity on Diversity and Inclu sion 
Day.  The Troy High Orchestra Holiday Concert was 
High robotics team who won 2 nd  place.  Pranathi also 
mentioned that they would be holding a canned food 
drive for Troy citizens.   
Ellory Thill, student representative from Athens Hi gh 
School announced that their Diversity and Inclusion Day 
was a huge success and they have a banner filled wi th 
students’ names.  Winter sports have begun and are 
going very well.  Ellory shared that the Band, Orch estra 
and Choir are putting on annual holiday concerts an d 
that student council will be meeting to introduce Charity 
Week.  Ellory commented that they collected 7,000 
cans in their recent food drive.

9.        PERSONNEL 
A. CONSIDERATION: Employment Dispute Resolution (EDR) Plan 
The purpose of the Troy School District Employment Dispute Resolution Plan (the 
“EDR Plan”) shall be to provide for the fair, efficient, and timely resolution o f all 
claims, disputes or controversies arising out of or relating to an application or 
candidacy for employment, employment and/or cessation of employment, and to 
provide an applicant or employee with a forum in which claims or disputes with 
the District may be resolved exclusively by final and binding arbitration rather 
than litigation.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: _______________________________ 
Supported By:   
WHEREAS, the Board of Ed ucation of the Troy School District  has determined 
that it is in the best interest of the District to establish an Employment Dispute 
Resolution Plan applicable to all applicants for employment, al l substitute 
employees, all nonunion employees and all non union administrators and 
supervisors employed by the School District; and 
WHEREAS, the Revised School Code, MCL 380.11a permits a school district to 
establish an Employment Dispute Resolution Plan. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 1. The Board hereb y authorizes the establishment of an Employment 
Dispute Resolution Plan applicable to all applicants for employment, al l substitute 
employees, all nonunion employees and all non union administrators and 
supervisors employed by the School District. 
 2. The Superintendent or his designee is hereby authorized to execute 
the Plan documents and any necessary related documents for the Board. 
 3. All resolutions and parts of resolutions insofar as they conflict with 
the provisions of this resolution be and the same hereby are rescinded. 
Yes: __________ 
No:  __________ 
126
TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
EMPLOYMENT DISPUTE RESOLUTION PLAN 
1. Purpose of the Plan 
The purpose of the Troy School District (the “District”) Employment Dispute Resolution 
Plan (the “EDR Plan”) shall be to provide for the fair, efficient, and timely resolution of  
all claims, disputes or controversies arising out of or relating to an application or 
candidacy for employment, employment and/or cessation of employment , and to provide 
an applicant or employee with a forum in which claims or disputes with the District may 
be resolved exclusively by final and binding arbitration rather than litigation. This EDR 
Plan does not restrict an applicant or employee from filing a claim or charge with any 
state, federal or other governmental administrative agency. Rather, this EDR Plan applies 
only to local, state or federal court proceedings. 
2. Individuals Subject to the Plan 
All applicants or candidates for employment , and all employees not represented by a 
bargaining unit shall be subject to the terms of this  EDR Plan. For purposes of this EDR 
Plan, the terms “employee” and “employees” refer to current employees, as well as 
applicants or candidates for employment. 
3. Disputes Subject to the Plan 
Any controversy or claim arising out of or relating to a covered employee’s employment 
or a candidate or applicant’s application for employment, or employment contract, shall 
be settled by arbitration administered by the American Arbitration Association  (“AAA”). 
Michigan’s Uniform Arbitration Act (“UAA”), MCL 691.1681 et seq ., and AAA  
Employment Arbitration Rules and Mediation Procedures  (“AAA Employment Rules”) 
shall govern such arbitrations . Judgment upon the award rendered by the arbitrator(s) 
may be entered in any court of competent jurisdiction.  
a. Collective Actions Waiver 
Employees must individually arbitrate employment disputes except where 
collective action is expressly permitted by federal law. 
4. Initiation of a Claim 
a. Statute of Limitations 
An employee wishing to initiate arbitration of an employment dispute pursuant to this 
EDR Plan must file a Demand for Arbitration on the AAA’s website or with the AAA 
Detroit Regional Office, located at 39555 Orchard Hill Place, Suite 600, Novi, M ichigan 
48375. Demands for A rbitration must be filed within the time limit established by the 
applicable statute of limitations if the dispute involves statutory rights . If no statutory 
127
rights are involved, the time limit for filing claims shall be thirty ( 30) calendar days from 
the date that mediation was terminated pursuant to AAA National Rules for the 
Resolution of Employment Disputes or thirty (30) calendar days from the date the dispute 
arose or the employee knew, or should have known that the dispute arose. 
Nothing in this EDR Plan prohibits an employee from filing a complaint(s) with a 
federal, state or other governmental administrative agency. 
5. Notice of Plan Implementation 
Employees shall be notified at least thirty (30) calendar days prior to implementation of 
the EDR Plan. 
6. Cost-Sharing of Arbitration Fees 
a. Filing Fee 
Employees shall be required to pay fifty percent (50%) of the arbitration filing 
fee.  
b. Financial Hardship 
In cases where the employee is able to demonstrate financial hardship and an 
inability to pay the employee’s portion of the filing fee, the arbitrator shall have 
the authority to order the District to reimburse the employee for all or a portion of 
the employee’s share of the arbitration filing fee. 
c. Hearing Fees 
Hearing fees shall be paid in accordance with the AAA Employment Rules. 
d. Hearing Room Rental  
The hearing fees described above do not cover the rental of hearing rooms. The 
AAA maintains hearing rooms in most offices for the convenience of the parties. 
Hearing room rental fees will be borne by the employer.  
e. Abeyance Fee  
Parties on cases held in abeyance for one year will be assessed an annual 
abeyance fee of $300. If a party refuses to pay the assessed fee, the other party or 
parties may pay the entire fee on behalf of all parties, otherwise the matter will be 
administratively closed.  
2 
128
f. Expenses  
The costs relating to proof and witnesses produced at the direction of the 
arbitrator shall be shared equally by the employer and employee. All other 
expenses of the arbitrator , including required travel and other expenses, shall be 
borne by the employer.  
g. Award of Fees 
The arbitrator has the authority to award fees when the employee is the claimant 
party, when such an award is in accordance with applicable law and the interes ts 
of justice. 
7. Compensation of the Arbitrator 
Impartiality is best assumed by the parties sharing the fees and expenses of the mediator 
and arbitrator. In cases where the economic condition of a party does not permit equal 
sharing, the parties should make mutually acceptable arrangements to achieve that goal, if 
at all possible. In the absence of such an agreement, the arbitrator shall determine 
allocation of fees. The AAA shall facilitate the negotiation of the parties’ share of costs 
and collection of such fees so as to reduce the bias of potential disparate contributions 
and shall forward payment to the mediator and/or arbitrator without disclosing the 
parties’ respective share of the payment. 
8. Neutral  Employment Dispute Resolution Plan Administrator 
The AAA is designated to administer the external compone nt of this E DR Plan 
(mediation and arbitration processes) . The AAA Employment Rules shall govern the 
mediation and arbitration processes. 
9. Due Process Protocol 
This EDR Plan shall adhere to th ose due process safeguards required by the UAA, MCL 
691.1681 et seq., and, where a dispute or claim relates to an employee’s statutory rights, 
the safeguards developed by the AAA Task Force on Alternative Dispute Resolution. 
a. Representation 
Employees using the mediation and/or arbitration procedures of this EDR Plan 
shall have the right to be represented by a spokesperson of their own choosing . 
The employee may seek such representation through various organizations, 
including but not limited to, bar associations, legal service associations, civil 
rights organizations, trade unions, etc. 
3 
129
b. Fees for Representation 
The amount and method of payment for representation should be determined 
between the employee claimant and her/his representative . In cases where an 
employee is able to demonstrate financial hardship and an inability to pay fees for 
representation, the arbitrator shall have authority to provide for fee reimbursement 
where the employee is the prevailing party . The arbitrator shall have authority to 
provide for fee reimbursement in whole or in part in accordance wi th applicable 
law or in the interests of justice. 
c. Access to Information 
The parties shall have adequate but limited pre -trial discovery , as provided by 
MCL 691.1697. Necessary pre-hearing depositions consistent with the expedited 
nature of arbitration shall be available. 
d. Mediator and Arbitrator Qualification 
The roster of mediators and arbitrators shall contain representatives with training 
regarding statutes, including substantive, procedural and remedial issues to be 
confronted in statutory rights employment disputes, skills in conducting hearings 
and knowledge of the statutory environment in which statutory rights disputes 
arise. Nothing in this EDR Plan shall prohibit the parties from jointly selecting as 
mediator and/or arbitrator one in whom bot h parties have requisite trust . Disputes 
shall be mediated by a single mediator. Arbitrations shall be conducted by a single 
arbitrator.  
e. Conflicts of Interest 
The mediator and/or arbitrator for an employment dispute shall disclose any 
relationship which  might reasonably constitute or be perceived as a conflict of 
interest. The designated mediator and/or arbitrator shall sign an oath provided by 
AAA affirming the absence of such present or preexisting ties. 
10. Authority of Arbitrator 
The arbitrator shall b e bound by the Arbitration Agreement signed by the parties, applicable 
statutes and regulations, the UAA, and the AAA Employment Rules , with respect to the 
arbitrator’s authority to determine the time and place of the hearing, permit reasonable discovery, 
issue subpoenas, decide arbitrability issues, preserve order and privacy in the hearings, rule on 
evidentiary matters, determine the close of the hearing and procedures for post -hearing 
submissions, and issue an award resolving the submitted dispute. 
4 
130
11. Scope of Review 
Pursuant to Michigan’s UAA, MCL 691.1702, either party may move for an order 
confirming the arbitration award in a circuit court of competent jurisdiction. The 
arbitrator’s award shall be final and binding and subject to limited judicial review as 
provided in MCL 691.1703.  
I understand the terms of this EDR Plan and acknowledge receipt thereof.

      (Employee Name)

      (Date)

Proposals For Purchase of Real Property (“RFP”) to obtain proposals to purchase

B.  CONSIDERATION:  Portable Testing Tables and Chairs 
A RFP was issued to obtain competitive bids for portable testing tables 
and chairs.  Bid responses were reviewed by Todd Hensley, Purchasing 
Supervisor, and it is recommended the bid be awarded as stated below.  
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
 Be it therefore resolved that the bid for portable testing tables and chairs 
be awarded to Global Equipment Co., Inc.  in the amount of $101,162.00 
and that this amount be approved as an expenditure of the general fund. 
 Yes: 
 No: 
134
Memorandum 
          TO:     Rick West – Assistant Superintendent  
                      Business Services   
    FROM:     Todd Hensley – Purchasing Supervisor  
Purchasing Department 
Troy, Michigan  48093 
                      Equipment - Athens and Troy High Schools                        
Phone  248 823-4091 
Upon receiving se veral price quotes during r esearch on portable tables and chairs for  
mandatory annual testing at both Athens and Troy High Schools , it became apparent that the 
cost of this testing equipment was above the Michigan Department of Education’s Competitive 
Bid Threshold; hence, a formal bid was processed.  
Invitations were sent to nine potential bidders to provide closed-sealed pricing for the required 
testing equipment; twelve responses were received. After analyzing the information provided 
in the proposals, the lowest qualified bidder was Global Equipment Co, Inc.   
 It is therefore recommended that Global Equipment Co, Inc., be awarded BID 9831 – Portable 
Tables and Chairs – Testing Equipment in the amount of $ 101,162.00, as detailed in the 
attached bid tabulation.
